The party's ideology has been broadly described by scholars, including [James Shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Shields_\(academic\)), [Nonna Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonna_Mayer), [Jean-Yves Camus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Yves_Camus), [Nicolas Lebourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Lebourg) and [Michel Winock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Winock) as [nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist), [far-right](221220.md) (or *[Nouvelle Droite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouvelle_Droite)*) and [populist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Populist).[^cite_note-203] Jean-Yves Camus and Nicolas Lebourg, following [Pierre-André Taguieff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Andr%C3%A9_Taguieff)'s analysis, include the party in an old French tradition of "national populism" that can be traced back to [Boulangism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_Ernest_Boulanger). National populists combine the social values of the left and the political values of the right and advocate a [referendary](51582.md) republic that would bypass traditional political divisions and institutions. Aiming at unity of the political (the *demos*), ethnic (the *ethnos*), and social (the *working class*) interpretations of the "people", they claim to defend the "average Frenchman" and "common sense", against the "betrayal of inevitably corrupt elites".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECamusLebourg201712–13-204] The party has been also described as [national conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_conservatism).[^cite_note-205]

The FN has changed considerably since its foundation, pursuing the principles of modernisation and pragmatism and adapting to the changing political climate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007309-206][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeClair1999115-207] Its message increasingly influenced mainstream political parties,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeClair1999115-207][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007312-208] and some commentators described it as right-wing, moving closer towards the centre-right.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007313-209][^cite_note-215] In the 2010s, the party attempted to "de-demonise" its image and changed its name to National Rally. A 2022 Kanar survey found that 46% of French voters saw Marine Le Pen as "representing a patriotic Right attached to traditional values", although 50% saw her as "a danger to democracy".[^cite_note-\:5-216]

### Society

#### Gender and sexuality

The party opposed the 2016 criminalisation of [prostitution in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prostitution_in_France) on the grounds that it would negatively affect the safety of sex workers.[^cite_note-217]

In the [2002 legislative elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_French_legislative_election), the first under the new gender parity provision in the French Constitution, Le Pen's [National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Front_\(France\)) was among the few parties to come close to meeting the law, with 49% female candidates; Jospin's [Socialists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_\(France\)) had 36%, and Chirac's [UMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_a_Popular_Movement) had 19.6%.[^cite_note-218] Women voters in France were traditionally more attracted to mainstream conservative parties than the radical right until the 2000s. The proportion of women in the party has risen to 39% by 2017.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECamusLebourg2017202-219]

#### Law and order

In 2002, Jean-Marie Le Pen campaigned on a [law-and-order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_and_order_\(politics\)) platform of [zero tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero_tolerance), harsher sentencing, increased prison capacity, and a [referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Referendums_in_France) on re-introducing the [death penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_France).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007282-137] In its 2001 programme, the party linked the breakdown of law and order to immigration, deeming immigration a "mortal threat to civil peace in France."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007284-139]

Marine Le Pen rescinded the party's traditional support for the death penalty with her 2017 campaign launch, instead announcing support for imprisonment "in perpetuity" for the "worst crimes" in February 2017.[^cite_note-220] In 2022, she proposed to hold a [referendum](51582.md) on [capital punishment in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_France) if she were elected.[^cite_note-\:4-221]

### Immigration

Since its early years, the party has called for immigration to be reduced.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007177–185-222] The theme of exclusion of non-European immigrants was brought into the party in 1978 and became increasingly important in the 1980s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKitscheltMcGann199795-223]

After the 1999 split, the FN cultivated a more moderate image on immigration and Islam, no longer calling for the systematic repatriation of legal immigrants but still supporting the deportation of illegal, criminal, or unemployed immigrants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007315-224]

Following the [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring) (2011) rebellions in several countries, Marine Le Pen campaigned for a halt to the migration of Tunisian and Libyan immigrants to Europe.[^cite_note-225]

In November 2015, the party stated its goal to have a [net legal immigration rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_migration_rate) (immigrants minus emigrants) of 10,000 in France per year. Since 2017, that yearly net immigration rate was around 182,000[^cite_note-226] if one takes into account only people born abroad from non-French parents, but was around 44,000 if one also includes the departures and returns of French expatriates.[^cite_note-227]

In 2022, Marine Le Pen proposed an end to "family reunification" rights for foreigners with residency permits and the end to the right to automatic citizenship for children born in France to foreigners living there.[^cite_note-\:5-216] She also supported a referendum on immigration policy.[^cite_note-\:4-221]

#### Islam

The National Rally has sought to restrict Islamic practice in France. In 2011, Marine Le Pen warned that wearing full-face veils is "the tip of the iceberg" of [Islamisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamisation) of French culture.[^cite_note-tnyt-228] In 2021,[^cite_note-229] and again in 2022, Le Pen again proposed banning the [hijab](68301.md) (headscarf which only covers the head but not face) in public.[^cite_note-halal-230] Le Pen also proposed to ban the production of both [halal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halal) and [kosher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosher) meat.[^cite_note-halal-230] Le Pen says she is "against the visibility of Islam" but not Islam per se.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

The National Rally is considered [Islamophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamophobic) by many.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234][^cite_note-halal-230][^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236] The party has connected immigration to Islamic terrorism.[^cite_note-237]

Paradoxically, [Mayotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayotte), an [overseas department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_department) on the Indian Ocean with an over-90% Muslim majority has been a stronghold for the party, and gave Marine Le Pen her highest vote percentage in the [2022 French presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election) first round.[^cite_note-BAMBA_2022-238][^cite_note-Saunders_2022-239] The political messaging in Mayotte attacks [Comorian](5403.md) immigrants, not Islam.[^cite_note-Torcu-240]

### Economy

At the end of the 1970s, Jean-Marie Le Pen broke away from the [anti-capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-capitalist) heritage of Poujadism and espoused a [market liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_liberal) and [anti-statist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-statism) programme which included lower taxes, reducing state intervention, reducing the size of the public sector, privatisation, and scaling back government bureaucracy. Some scholars have characterised the FN's 1978 programme as "[Reaganite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reaganomics) before [Reagan](25433.md)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKitscheltMcGann199795-223]

The party's economic policy shifted from the 1980s to the 1990s from [neoliberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberalism) to [protectionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism).[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007272-242] This occurred within the framework of a changed international environment, from a battle between the [Free World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_World) and [Communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_International), to one between [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_nationalism) and [globalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalisation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007275-127] During the 1980s, Jean-Marie Le Pen complained about the rising number of "social parasites" and called for deregulation, tax cuts, and the phasing-out of the [welfare state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_state).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007272-242] As the party gained growing support from the economically vulnerable, it converted towards politics of social welfare and economic protectionism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007272-242] This was part of its shift away from its former claim of being the "social, popular, and national right" to its claim of being "neither right nor left – French!"[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007274-243] Increasingly, the party's program became an amalgam of free market and welfare policies. By the 2010s, some political commentators described its economic policies as left-wing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007275-127][^cite_note-theconversation.com-244][^cite_note-245]

Under Marine Le Pen, the RN has supported [economic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_nationalism),[^cite_note-246] which it calls "economic patriotism", and it has advocated populist policies, such as tax cuts for people under 30 years old, and cuts in the [value-added tax](52177473.md) on energy and essential products. The party has supported public services, protectionism, and economic intervention and opposed the increase in the fuel tax in 2018 and the increase in the retirement age in 2023.[^cite_note-\:5-216][^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248]

Under Jordan Bardella, the RN has adopted more pro-market policies, including lower taxes and simplifying industrial norms.[^cite_note-249] Bardella advocated an audit of public finances as a precursor to determining the 2025 budget.[^cite_note-250] Bardella has sought to use these policies to court business support during the [2024 French legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_legislative_election) campaign.[^cite_note-251] During this time, Bardella also rescinded the prior RN pledge to repeal the [2023 French pension reform law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_pension_reform_law).[^cite_note-252] Bardella has been described as advocating [economic liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalism), putting him in conflict with the economic vision of Marine Le Pen.[^cite_note-253]

### Climate

Le Pen does not plan to withdraw from the [Paris Climate Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Climate_Agreement) but has stated that climate change is "not the priority" of the party.[^cite_note-presidential_program-254] The party is against measures to tackle climate change and protecting the environment.[^cite_note-255] The National Rally has proposed abolition of the [European Green Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Green_Deal), highlighting key policies it opposes such as the 2035 ban on fossil fuel cars.[^cite_note-256]

### Foreign policy

From the 1980s to the 1990s, the party's policy shifted from favouring the [European Union](9317.md) to turning against it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007272-242] In 2002, Jean-Marie Le Pen campaigned on pulling France out of the EU and re-introducing the [franc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franc) as the country's national currency.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007282-137] In the early 2000s, the party denounced the [Schengen](179592.md), [Maastricht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maastricht_Treaty), and [Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Amsterdam) treaties as foundations for "a supranational entity spelling the end of France."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007282_f-257] In 2004, the party criticised the EU as "the last stage on the road to [world government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_government)", likening it to a "puppet of the [New World Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_World_Order_\(conspiracy_theory\))."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007299-258] It also proposed breaking all institutional ties back to the [Treaty of Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Rome) while it returned to supporting a common European currency to rival the United States dollar.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007299-258] Further, it rejected the possible [accession of Turkey to the EU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007299-258] The FN was also one of several parties that backed France's 2005 [rejection of the Treaty for a European Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_French_European_Constitution_referendum). In other issues, Le Pen opposed the invasions of Iraq, led by the [United States](3434750.md), both in the 1991 [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) and the 2003 [Iraq War](5043324.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007315-224] He visited [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) in [Baghdad](4492.md) in 1990 and subsequently considered him a friend.[^cite_note-tnyt2-259]

Marine Le Pen advocated France leaving the [euro](9472.md), although that policy was dropped in 2019.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] She also intends to reintroduce [customs borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs_area) and has campaigned against allowing [dual citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_citizenship).[^cite_note-spiegel-262] During both the [2010–2011 Ivorian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%932011_Ivorian_crisis) and the [2011 Libyan civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Libyan_civil_war), she opposed the French military involvements.[^cite_note-tnyt-228] However, the party supported the 2013 [Operation Serval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Serval) in Mali against Islamist militants in the country because it was at the request of the Malian government.[^cite_note-263]

Le Pen has praised Egyptian President [Abdel Fattah El-Sisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel_Fattah_El-Sisi) for "fighting radical Islam", stating that Egypt's "ability to separate extremist Islam from the religion sets an example to the rest of the world, including France, of how to deal with poisonous ideologies".[^cite_note-264] The party has also favourably contrasted the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md)'s opposition to Islamism with the more pro-Islamist position taken by [Qatar](8238258.md).[^cite_note-265] The party has advocated closer [France–Morocco relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%E2%80%93Morocco_relations), criticising Macron's attempts to deepen ties with Algeria.[^cite_note-266] In January 2023, the National Rally was one of only four parties in the European Parliament that voted against a resolution condemning Morocco's treatment of journalist [Omar Radi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Radi).[^cite_note-267] It praised the 2024 recognition of Moroccan sovereignty over the [Western Sahara](33220.md) and suggested the French government should have acted sooner.[^cite_note-268]

Le Pen supports the restoration of [France-Syria relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France-Syria_relations) and called for cooperation with Israel, the United States, Russia, Iran, and Saudi Arabia to support the economic recovery of Lebanon from the [Lebanese economic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_economic_crisis_\(2019-present\)).

The party supports a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) to the [Israel-Palestine conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel-Palestine_conflict) and welcomed the [Abraham Accords](64883396.md).[^cite_note-presidential_program-254] The party has shifted towards more [pro-Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Israel) policies over time, particularly following the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271] Bardella has expressed opposition to recognition of Palestinian statehood following the [2023 Hamas-led attack on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel), on the grounds that this would be "recognising terrorism".[^cite_note-272] Following the [2024 Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon), Le Pen suggested that a ceasefire would be impossible without the "dismantling" of [Hezbollah](13919.md).[^cite_note-273] Following the [June 2025 Israeli strikes on Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Israeli_strikes_on_Iran), Bardella called for France to "stand alongside Israel" and claimed that Iran acquiring nuclear weapons would threaten world peace, while suggesting that France's role was to promote "peace and de-escalation".[^cite_note-274] The National Rally welcomed the subsequent [2025 United States strikes on Iranian nuclear sites](80262844.md),[^cite_note-275] but noted that strikes on Iran should be exclusively targeted at nuclear sites.[^cite_note-276]

#### Russia and Ukraine

In 2015, Marine Le Pen described Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) as a "defender of the Christian heritage of European civilisation."[^cite_note-Ind141127-277] The National Front considers that [Ukraine](31750.md) has been subjugated by the [United States](3434750.md), through the [Revolution of Dignity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity). The National Front denounces anti-Russian feelings in Eastern Europe and the submission of Western Europe to "Washington's" interests in the region.[^cite_note-278] Marine Le Pen is very critical against the threats of sanctions directed by the international community against Russia: "European countries should seek a solution through diplomacy rather than making threats that could lead to an escalation." She argues that the United States is leading a [new Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Cold_War) against Russia. She sees no other solution for peace in Ukraine than to organise a kind of federation that would allow each region a large degree of autonomy.[^cite_note-279] She thinks Ukraine should be sovereign and free as any other nation.[^cite_note-280]

[Luke Harding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Harding) wrote in *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* that the National Front's [MEPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Members_of_the_European_Parliament) were a "pro-Russian bloc."[^cite_note-GN141208-281] In 2014, the *[Nouvel Observateur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouvel_Observateur)* said that the Russian government considered the National Front "capable of seizing power in France and changing the course of European history in Moscow's favour."[^cite_note-NO141127-282] According to the French media, party leaders had frequent contact with Russian ambassador Alexander Orlov and Marine Le Pen made multiple trips to [Moscow](19004.md).[^cite_note-BD141123-283] In May 2015, one of her advisers, [Emmanuel Leroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Leroy?action=edit\&redlink=1), attended an event in Donetsk marking the "independence" of the self-proclaimed [Donetsk People's Republic](42425747.md).[^cite_note-DB150514-284]

The RN remains divided on relations with Russia, with Bardella stating that he believed Russia threatened French security. At the same time, [Thierry Mariani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Mariani) suggested it was not a threat to France or Europe.[^cite_note-285] The more pro-Russian Mariani has been described as in conflict with [Pierre-Romain Thionnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Romain_Thionnet), who has advocated a more pro-Ukrainian line, with Bardella maintaining a balance between the two positions.[^cite_note-286]

During the [2022 French presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election), Le Pen supported sending non-lethal defensive aid to Ukraine in the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), but not heavy weapons that would make France a "co-belligerent" in the conflict.[^cite_note-presidential_program-254] Similarly, Bardella has expressed support for defense equipment, ammunition, and logistical assistance to Ukraine but maintains opposition to giving long-range missiles to Ukraine or deploying French soldiers there.[^cite_note-287] He is also opposed to Ukrainian NATO membership, suggesting it could escalate the war.[^cite_note-288]

#### European Union

Since their entry into the European Parliament in 1979, the National Rally has promoted a message of being pro-Europe but anti-EU.[^cite_note-289] However, in 2019, the proposal that France leave the [Eurozone](184391.md) and the EU was removed from the party's manifesto, which has since called for "reform from within" of the union.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] The party advocates that EU legislation should be initiated by the [Council of the EU](5866.md) rather than the [European Commission](9974.md), and that French laws should have primacy over EU laws.[^cite_note-\:3-35][^cite_note-\:4-221]

#### NATO

The party's stance on NATO has varied throughout the years. Under Jean-Marie Le Pen's leadership, the party advocated a complete withdrawal from the organization, while under Marine Le Pen's leadership, the party has softened its stance to advocate leaving NATO's integrated military command structure, which France joined in 2009.[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella) later added that the RN would not advocate withdrawing France from the integrated command while the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) was ongoing.[^cite_note-297]

### Electoral reform and referendums

The National Rally has advocated for full [proportional representation](23298.md) in France, claiming that the [two-round system](25932.md) disenfranchises voters. In early 2021, Marine Le Pen, along with centrist politician [François Bayrou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Bayrou) and green politician [Julien Bayou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julien_Bayou), cosigned a letter asking President Emmanuel Macron to implement proportional representation for future elections.[^cite_note-298]

The party advocates referendums on key issues such as the death penalty, immigration policy, and constitutional change. In 2022, Marine Le Pen stated: "I want the referendum to become a classic operating tool."[^cite_note-\:4-221]

## Controversies

### Opinions on the Holocaust and relations with Jewish groups

There has been a difference between Marine Le Pen's and her father's opinions concerning the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust) and Jews. In 2005, Jean-Marie Le Pen wrote in the far-right weekly magazine *[Rivarol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivarol_\(magazine\))* that the German occupation of France "was not particularly inhumane, even if there were a few blunders, inevitable in a country of 640,000 square kilometres (250,000 sq. mi.)" and in 1987 referred to the [Nazi gas chambers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_gas_chambers) as "a point of detail of the history of the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War)". He has repeated the latter claim several times.[^cite_note-299] In 2004, Bruno Gollnisch said: "I do not question the existence of [concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_concentration_camps), but historians could discuss the number of deaths. As to the existence of gas chambers, it is up to historians to determine".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007308-300] Jean-Marie Le Pen was fined for these remarks, but Gollnisch was found not guilty by the [Court of Cassation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Cassation_\(France\)).[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-holocauste-302][^cite_note-303] The leader of the party, Marine Le Pen, distanced herself for a time from the party machine in protest at her father's comments.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007317-304] In response to her father's remarks, Marine Le Pen referred to the Holocaust as the "abomination of abominations".[^cite_note-305]

&#x20;During the 2012 presidential election, Marine Le Pen sought the support of Jewish people in France.[^cite_note-306] Interviewed by the [Israeli](9282173.md) [daily newspaper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_newspaper) *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* about the fact that some of her European senior colleagues had formed alliances with, and visited, some Israeli settlers and groups, Marine Le Pen said:&#x20;

> The shared concern about radical Islam explains the relationship ... but it is possible that behind it is also the need of the visitors from Europe to change their image in their countries ... As far as their partners in Israel are concerned, I myself don't understand the idea of continuing to develop the [settlements](15123.md). I consider it a political mistake and would like to make it clear in this context that we must have the right to [criticise the policy of the State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_the_Israeli_government) – just as we are allowed to criticise any sovereign country – without it being considered [anti-Semitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_France). After all, the National Front has always been [Zionistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism) and always defended Israel's right to exist.

&#x20;She has opposed the emigration of French Jews to Israel in response to radical Islam, explaining: "The Jews of France are Frenchmen, they're at home here, and they must stay here and not emigrate. The country is obligated to provide solutions against the development of radical Islam in problematic areas".[^cite_note-307]

### Czecho-Russian bank loan

In November 2014, Marine Le Pen confirmed that the party had received a €9 million loan from the First Czech Russian Bank (FCRB) in Moscow to the National Front.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-MP141127-309] Senior FN officials from the party's political bureau informed [Mediapart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediapart) that this was the first instalment of a €40 million loan, although Marine Le Pen has disputed this.[^cite_note-Ind141127-277][^cite_note-MP141127-309] *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)* said the loans "take Moscow's attempt to influence the internal politics of the EU to a new level".[^cite_note-Ind141127-277] [Reinhard Bütikofer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinhard_B%C3%BCtikofer) stated, "It's remarkable that a political party from the motherland of freedom can be funded by Putin's sphere—the largest European enemy of freedom".[^cite_note-DW141129-310] Marine Le Pen argued that it was not a [donation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donation) from the Russian government but a loan from a private Russian bank because no other bank would give her a loan. This loan is meant to prepare future electoral campaigns and to be repaid progressively. Marine Le Pen has publicly disclosed all the rejection letters that French banks have sent to her concerning her loan requests.[^cite_note-leparisien1-311] Since November 2014, she insists that if a French bank agrees to give her a loan, she would break her contract with the FCBR, but she has not received any other counter-propositions.[^cite_note-312] Le Pen accused the banks of collusion with the government.[^cite_note-leparisien1-311] In April 2015, a Russian hacker group published texts and emails between [Timur Prokopenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timur_Prokopenko), a member of Putin's administration, and [Konstantin Rykov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Rykov), a former Duma deputy with ties to France, discussing Russian financial support to the National Front in exchange for its support of [Russia's annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation), though this has not coalesced.[^cite_note-Monde150403-313]

### Identitarian links

A 2019 undercover investigation by [Al Jazeera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazeera_Arabic) uncovered links between high-ranking National Rally figures and [Generation Identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generation_Identity), a far-right [Identitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identitarian) youth group. In secretly taped conversations, RN leaders endorsed goals of Generation Identity and discussed plans to "remigrate" immigrants, effectively sending them back to their countries of origin, if the RN came to power. [Christelle Lechevalier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christelle_Lechevalier), a RN Member of the European Parliament (MEP), said that many RN leaders held similar opinions as the GI, but sought to hide them from voters.[^cite_note-GenId-314]

### Payment of party officials with EU funds

Main article: [National Rally assistants affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally_assistants_affair)

In December 2023, 28 people, including [Marine Le Pen](572897.md) and her father [Jean Marie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Marie_Le_Pen), were ordered to stand trial after they were charged with a scheme which involved paying National Rally party officials through [EU](9317.md) funds which were earmarked for [European Parliament](9581.md) assistants.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316]

On 31 March 2025, 25 National Rally members (including Le Pen, 9 other former NR MEPs, and 12 assistants) were found guilty. The sentences for Le Pen and several former MEPs included bans from running for political office.[^cite_note-Gdn31325-28][^cite_note-TelegraphConviction-29][^cite_note-France24Conviction-30]

Le Pen has denied the allegations. Her appeal against both the verdict and sentence was heard at a Paris appeals court in early 2026. On July 7, the verdict was upheld, with a number of revisions and clarifications of the sentence, including the wearing of an [ankle location monitor](87233.md). The ruling would allow Le Pen to run for office.[^cite_note-Gdnappeal-317][^cite_note-France24appeal-318]<https://www.bbc.com/news/articles/cly85qjg45no>

## Organization

### Leadership

The executive bureau features: [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella) (president), [Steeve Briois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steeve_Briois) (vice-president), [Louis Aliot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Aliot) (vice-president), [David Rachline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Rachline) (vice-president), [Kévin Pfeffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%A9vin_Pfeffer) (treasurer), [Julien Sanchez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julien_Sanchez) (spokesperson), [Gilles Pennelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilles_Pennelle) (regional councilor), [Edwige Diaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwige_Diaz) (deputy regional councilor), [Hélène Laporte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A9l%C3%A8ne_Laporte), [Philippe Olivier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_Olivier), and [Jean-Paul Garraud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Paul_Garraud).[^cite_note-319]

#### Presidents

| No | President | Term start | Term end |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | [Jean-Marie Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Marie_Le_Pen) | 5 October 1972 | 15 January 2011 |
| Jean-Marie Le Pen founded the *National Front for French Unity* party in 1972 and contested the Presidency of France in [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_French_presidential_election), [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_French_presidential_election), [1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_French_presidential_election), [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_French_presidential_election) and [2007](11105698.md). He served several terms as a deputy of the [National Assembly of France](299616.md) and a [Member of the European Parliament](258909.md). He later served as the party's honorary president from January 2011 to August 2015.[^cite_note-\:0-320] He died on 7 January 2025 at the age of 96.[^cite_note-321] |  |  |  |
| 2 | [Marine Le Pen](572897.md) | 15 January 2011 | 5 November 2022 |
| Marine Le Pen took over as the president of the party in 2011 and contested the [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_French_presidential_election), [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_French_presidential_election) and [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election) French presidential elections. She served as a [Member of the European Parliament](258909.md) from 2004 to 2017 and has served as a deputy of the [National Assembly of France](299616.md) since 2017. Under her leadership, the party was renamed *National Rally* in 2018. |  |  |  |
| 3 | [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella) | 5 November 2022 *Acting since 13 September 2021* | Incumbent |
| Jordan Bardella became acting president of the party after Marine Le Pen launched her presidential campaign in September 2021.[^cite_note-322] He was elected president in November 2022. |  |  |  |

### Elected representatives

As of February 2023, the National Rally has 88 [MPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_\(France\)). They sit in the [National Assembly](299616.md) as members of the [National Rally group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally_group_\(National_Assembly\)).

### International affiliation and relations

#### 1980s–2000s

The FN has been part of several groups in the [European Parliament](9581.md). The first group it helped co-establish was the [European Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Right_\(1984%E2%80%931989\)) after the 1984 election, which also consisted of the [Italian Social Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Social_Movement) (MSI), its early inspiration, and the Greek [National Political Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Political_Union_\(Greece\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007198-331] Following the 1989 election, it teamed up with the German [Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_\(Germany\)) and the Belgian [Vlaams Blok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlaams_Blok) in a new [European Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Right_\(1989%E2%80%931994\)) group, while the MSI left due to the Germans' arrival.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeClair1999193-332] As the MSI evolved into the [National Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Alliance_\(Italy\)), it chose to distance itself from the FN.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDeClair1999194-333]

From 1999 to 2001, the FN was a member of the [Technical Group of Independents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technical_Group_of_Independents_\(1999%E2%80%932001\)). In 2007, it was part of the short-lived [Identity, Tradition, Sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity,_Tradition,_Sovereignty) group. Between the mentioned groups, the party sat among the non-affiliated [Non-Inscrits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Inscrits). It was part of the [Identity and Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity_and_Democracy) group.

It was formerly known as the Europe of Nations and Freedom group, during which time it also included the Polish [Congress of the New Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_the_New_Right), a former member of the [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party) and a former member of Romania's [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(Romania\)). The RN has also been part of the [Identity and Democracy Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identity_and_Democracy_Party) (formerly the Movement for a Europe of Nations and Freedom) since 2014, which additionally includes Slovakia's [We Are Family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Are_Family_\(Slovakia\)) and the Bulgarian [Volya Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volya_Movement), which was later renamed [Patriots.eu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriots.eu). After the [2024 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election), the National Rally joined the [Patriots for Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriots_for_Europe) group with Fidesz, Vox, the Czech [ANO 2011](40808475.md), the Portuguese [Chega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chega_\(political_party\)), the Greek [Voice of Reason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_Reason_\(political_party\)), [Latvia First](68481421.md) and most former ID members, with Bardella ultimately chairing the group.[^cite_note-334]

During Jean-Marie Le Pen's presidency, the party has also been active in establishing extra-parliamentary confederations. During the FN's 1997 national congress, the FN established the loose [Euronat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronat) group, which consisted of a variety of European [right-wing](56522.md) parties. Having failed to cooperate in the European Parliament, Le Pen sought in the mid-1990s to initiate contacts with other far-right parties, including from non-EU countries. The FN drew most support in Central and Eastern Europe, and Le Pen visited the Turkish [Welfare Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_Party). The significant [Freedom Party of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Party_of_Austria) (FPÖ) refused to join the efforts, as [Jörg Haider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B6rg_Haider) sought to distance himself from Le Pen, and later attempted to build a separate group.[^cite_note-tnyt2-259][^cite_note-335] In 2009, the FN joined the [Alliance of European National Movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_European_National_Movements); it left the alliance since. Along with some other European parties, the FN in 2010 visited Japan's *[Issuikai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issuikai)* ("right-wing") movement and the [Yasukuni Shrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasukuni_Shrine).[^cite_note-336]

#### 2010s

At a conference in 2011, the two new leaders of the FN and the FPÖ, [Marine Le Pen](572897.md) and [Heinz-Christian Strache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinz-Christian_Strache), announced deeper cooperation between their parties.[^cite_note-337] Pursuing her de-demonisation policy, in October 2011, Marine Le Pen, as new president of the National Front, joined the [European Alliance for Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Alliance_for_Freedom) (EAF).[^cite_note-338] The EAF is a pan-European sovereigntist platform founded late 2010 that is recognised by the European Parliament. The EAF has individual members linked to the Austrian Freedom Party of [Heinz-Christian Strache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinz-Christian_Strache), the [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party), and other movements such as the [Sweden Democrats](65873.md), [Vlaams Belang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlaams_Belang) (Belgian Flanders), Germany ([Bürger in Wut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizens_in_Rage)), and Slovakia ([Slovak National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovak_National_Party)).[^cite_note-339]

During her 2012 visit to the United States, Marine Le Pen met two [Republican](32070.md) members of the [U.S. House of Representatives](19468510.md) associated with the [Tea Party movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_Party_movement), [Joe Walsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Walsh_\(Illinois_politician\)), who is known for his strong stance against [Islam](6037917.md), which Domenic Powell argues, rises to [Islamophobia](162173.md)[^cite_note-340] and three-time presidential candidate [Ron Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Paul), whom Le Pen complimented for his stance on the [gold standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_standard).[^cite_note-341] In February 2017, two more conservative Republican Congressmen, [Steve King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_King) and [Dana Rohrabacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Rohrabacher), also met with Le Pen in Paris.[^cite_note-342]

The party also has ties to [Steve Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Bannon), who served as [White House Chief Strategist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Chief_Strategist) under President [Donald Trump](4848272.md), and addressed an RN event in 2018.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344]

The FN allied with the Dutch [Party for Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_for_Freedom) (PVV) in November 2013, after years of the PVV rejecting collaboration with the FN.[^cite_note-345] Similarly, in December 2013, the FN formed an alliance with [Matteo Salvini](1767165.md), the new leader of the [Lega Nord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lega_Nord), which had previously eschewed cooperation with the FN when it was led by [Umberto Bossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umberto_Bossi).[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347]

In 2014, [UK Independence Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_Independence_Party) leader [Nigel Farage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigel_Farage) was critical of the FN, claiming that the FN's association with "anti-Semitism and general prejudice" made it impossible for UKIP to join Le Pen's efforts for a united right-wing populist European Parliament group[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349] despite an invitation from PVV leader Wilders.[^cite_note-350]

In 2017, Marine Le Pen met with and was interviewed for the British radio station [LBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LBC) by Farage, who praised Le Pen and expressed support for her presidential bid.[^cite_note-351] Prior to the [2019 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_European_Parliament_election), Farage's [Brexit Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brexit_Party) initially considered forming a joint group in the next European Parliament, but ultimately once again declined.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353][^cite_note-EUalliance-354]

Though the FN had close contacts with [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md)'s [Brothers of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothers_of_Italy) (FdI) from 2013 onwards,[^cite_note-355] the relationship between the RN and FdI became strained in 2022, after Meloni publicly declined to support Le Pen's 2022 presidential bid.[^cite_note-356]

In 2023, Meloni complained to French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) after he compared her to Le Pen,[^cite_note-357] while Le Pen criticised Meloni's illegal immigration policies.[^cite_note-358]

However, in early 2024, Le Pen and Meloni made overtures to one another, declining to rule out future cooperation between their parties.[^cite_note-359] In July 2024, Meloni praised Le Pen's alliance with [Eric Ciotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Ciotti) and [Marion Maréchal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_Mar%C3%A9chal) during the 2024 French legislative election, congratulated the RN on its success in the first round of the election, and expressed preference for the right-wing alliance in the second round of the election.[^cite_note-360]

In addition, the party has had relations with [Krasimir Karakachanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasimir_Karakachanov)'s [IMRO – Bulgarian National Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMRO_%E2%80%93_Bulgarian_National_Movement) in 2014[^cite_note-361] and [Nenad Popović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nenad_Popovi%C4%87)'s [Serbian People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_People's_Party_\(2014\)) since 2021.[^cite_note-362] The RN was critical of the decision to allow the Bulgarian [Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revival_\(Bulgarian_political_party\)) to join the ID Party in 2024.[^cite_note-363]

Since 2018, the RN has had relations with [Santiago Abascal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Abascal)'s [Vox](41928488.md) in Spain.[^cite_note-364] In 2024, Vox had Le Pen address its conference, despite Vox being a member of the [European Conservatives and Reformists Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Group); after the election, Vox joined the RN in [Patriots for Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriots_for_Europe).[^cite_note-365]

In 2019, RN MEPs participated in the first international delegation to visit India's [Jammu and Kashmir](61449812.md) following the decision by [Narendra Modi](444222.md)'s [Bharatiya Janata Party](149330.md) government to [revoke the special status of Jammu and Kashmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revocation_of_the_special_status_of_Jammu_and_Kashmir). The delegation was not sanctioned by the European Parliament, and consisted mostly of right-wing populist politicians including MEPs from Vox, AfD, the Northern League, Vlaams Belang, the British [Brexit Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brexit_Party), and Poland's [Law and Justice](520309.md) party.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367]

#### 2020s

In October 2021, Le Pen met with [Fidesz](404647.md) leader and Hungarian Prime Minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md), Polish Prime Minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md) from the [Law and Justice](520309.md) (PiS) party, and [Slovenian Democratic Party](425720.md) leader and Slovenian Prime Minister [Janez Janša](1038986.md).[^cite_note-368] Both PiS and Fidesz had previously rejected cooperation with Le Pen in 2019.[^cite_note-EUalliance-354] Orbán subsequently supported Le Pen during the [2022 French presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election).[^cite_note-369] Morawiecki later expressed openness to the RN joining the [European Conservatives and Reformists Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Group) in February 2024.[^cite_note-370]

Relations with the AfD deteriorated in early 2024, following Le Pen's disagreements with the AfD members' discussions over [remigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remigration)[^cite_note-371] and the AfD questioning French control of [Mayotte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayotte).[^cite_note-372] In May 2024, the RN announced it would end its alliance with the AfD in the next European Parliament term.[^cite_note-373]

During the [2024 French legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_legislative_election), Israeli minister [Amichai Chikli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amichai_Chikli) expressed support for Le Pen and the National Rally, and suggested that [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) leader and Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) shared his views, prompting a rebuke by President Macron.[^cite_note-374] Chikli later described his relations with the RN, stating it was "natural that conservative leaders will have good relations with conservative leaders all across the globe".[^cite_note-375]

During and after the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md), the National Rally distanced itself from Trump, banning its MPs from commenting on the election.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379]

In 2024, Nigel Farage, in his capacity as the leader of [Reform UK](59757202.md), distanced himself from the RN, describing its economic agenda as a "disaster" for France.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] However, in December 2025, Farage met Bardella, with Bardella advocating for a "patriotic alliance" with Reform UK and expressing support for Farage becoming the next [British Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Prime_Minister).[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] Bardella later addressed the Reform UK conference in September 2026,[^cite_note-385] where he signed a Memorandum of Understanding between the National Rally and Reform UK, pledging future cooperation on illegal migration.[^cite_note-386]

## Election results

The National Front was a marginal party in 1973, the first election it participated in, but the party made its breakthrough in the 1984 European Parliament election, where it won 11% of the vote and ten MEPs. Following this election, the party's support mostly ranged from around 10 to 15%, although it saw a drop to around 5% in some late 2000s elections. Since 2010, the party's support seems to have increased towards its former heights. The party managed to advance to the final round of the 2002 French presidential election, although it failed to attract much more support after the initial first round vote. In the late 2000s the party suffered decline in elections. Under Marine Le Pen's presidency the party has increased its vote share significantly. The National Front came first in a national election for the first time during the [2014 European elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_European_Parliament_election_in_France), when it gained 24% of the vote. During the [2017 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_French_presidential_election) the party advanced to the second round of the election for the second time, and doubled the percentage it received in the 2002 presidential election, earning 34%. In the [2019 European elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_European_Parliament_election_in_France) the rebranded National Rally retained its spot as first party.

Le Pen reached the second round of the [2017 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_French_presidential_election), receiving 33.9% of the votes in the run-off and losing to [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md). Again in the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_presidential_election), she lost to Macron in the run-off, receiving 41.45% of the votes. In the [2022 parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_legislative_elections), the National Rally achieved a significant increase in the number of its MPs in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_National_Assembly), from 7 to 89 seats. In June 2024, the party won the [European Parliament elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election) in a landslide with 31.4% of the votes. This caused Macron to announce a snap election. Later that month, an RN-led right-wing coalition topped the first round of the [snap French legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_legislative_election) with a record 33.2% of the votes. On 7 July, the RN also won the popular vote (37.06%) in the second round of the snap election, but only won the third highest number of seats.[^cite_note-387]

[^cite_note-72]: &#x20;In France, parties have to secure support from a specific number of elected officials, from a specific number of departments, in order to be eligible to run for election. In 1976, the number of required elected officials was increased fivefold from the 1974 presidential cycle, and the number of departments threefold.<sup>[\[68\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007182-71)</sup>

[^cite_note-85]: &#x20;The 1984 European election used proportional representation system.

[^cite_note-91]: &#x20;The system had been designed by Mitterand's party to soften its predicted weakening in the polls.<sup>[\[85\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007209-90)</sup>

[^cite_note-112]: &#x20;If the election had used proportional representation, it would have won 64 seats.

[^cite_note-117]: &#x20;It had won a mayorship only once before, in the small town of [Saint-Gilles-du-Gard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Gilles-du-Gard) in 1989.<sup>[\[109\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShields2007261-116)</sup>

[^cite_note-176]: &#x20;The proportional representation was used in the 1986 elections.
